L2137: ## 7.3. Study 3002
L2138: Study 3002 was a phase 3, multicenter, randomized, double-blind, placebo and active comparator-controlled study in patients with moderate to severe plaque-type psoriasis who were candidates for either systemic therapy or phototherapy and may have received some systemic therapies or phototherapy for psoriasis previously.
L2139: ## 7.3.1. Key Inclusion criteria
L2140: Inclusion criteria were similar to Study 2001.
L2141: ## 7.3.2. Key Exclusion criteria
L2142: Exclusion criteria were similar to Study 2001.
L2143: ## 7.3.3. Treatment Groups and Study Design
L2144: The study consisted of 3 phases:
L2145: - Placebo- and active-comparator-controlled treatment (Week 0 to Week 24)
- Randomized withdrawal and retreatment (Week 28 through Week 72)
- Open-label guselkumab treatment (Week 76 through Week 160)
L2146: At Week 0, eligible subjects were randomized in a 2:1:1 fashion to 1 of 3 treatment groups:
L2147: - Group I: Guselkumab 100 mg at Weeks 0, 4, 12, and 20
- Group II: Placebo beginning at Week 0 followed by guselkumab 100 mg at Weeks 16 and 20
- Group III: Adalimumab (80 mg at Week 0 followed by adalimumab 40 mg at Week 1 and q2w thereafter through Week 23)
L2148: At Week 28, the randomized withdrawal and retreatment period began and continued through Week 72. Therapy during this time period was based on their level of response at Week 28:
L2149: - Subjects in Group I, randomized to guselkumab were treated as follows:Group Ia: PASI 90 nonresponders at Week 28 continued on guselkumab 100 mg q8w beginning at Week 28
- PASI90 responders at Week 28 were rerandomized in a 1:1 ratio to:Group Ib: Guselkumab 100 mg q8w beginning at Week 28
L2150: - Group Ic: Placebo beginning at Week 28. Upon loss of \ge 50\% of the improvement in PASI achieved at Week 28, subjects were retreated with guselkumab 100 mg followed by a 100 mg dose 4 weeks later, and then guselkumab 100 mg q8w thereafter
- Subjects in Group II, randomized to placebo, were treated as follows:Group IIa: PASI 90 nonresponders at Week 28 continued on guselkumab 100 mg q8w beginning at Week 28.
- Group IIb: PASI 90 responders at Week 28 received placebo beginning at Week 28. If subjects lost \ge 50\% of the improvement in PASI achieved at Week 28, subjects were retreated with guselkumab 100 mg followed by a 100 mg dose 4 weeks later, and then guselkumab 100 mg q8w thereafter.
- Subjects in Group III, randomized to adalimumab, were treated as follows:Group IIIa: PASI 90 nonresponders at Week 28 initiated guselkumab 100 mg at Week 28, followed by a 100 mg dose 4 weeks later, and then 100 mg q8w thereafter.
- Group IIIb: PASI 90 responders at Week 28 received placebo beginning at Week 28. Upon loss of \ge 50\% of the improvement in PASI achieved at Week 28, subjects initiated guselkumab 100 mg followed by a 100 mg dose 4 weeks later, and then guselkumab 100 mg q8w thereafter.
L2151: The open-label guselkumab treatment period began at Week 76 and continued through Week 160. Subjects received open-label guselkumab q8w through Week 148. A follow-up safety visit was scheduled at Week 160.
L2152: Database locks were planned for Weeks 48 and 160.
L2153: Figure 5 summarizes the study design.
L2154: Figure 5. Study Design (3002)
L2155: (Clinical Study Report, page 30 of 1120)
L2156: ## 7.3.4. Disposition
L2157: A total of 993 subjects were enrolled in this study and 992 subjects were treated at Week 0, including 496 subjects in the guselkumab group, 248 subjects in the placebo group, and 248 subjects in the adalimumab group. Disposition is summarized in Figures 6 – 8.

L2214: ## 7.4. Study 3003
L2215: Study 3003 was a phase 3, randomized, double-blind, multicenter study evaluating the efficacy and safety of guselkumab for the treatment of subjects with moderate to severe plaque-type psoriasis and an inadequate (IGA \ge 2) response to ustekinumab at Week 16.
L2216: ## 7.4.1. Key Inclusion Criteria
L2217: Inclusion criteria were similar to Study 2001.
L2218: ## 7.4.2. Key Exclusion Criteria
L2219: Exclusion criteria were similar to Study 2001.
L2220: ## 7.4.3. Treatment Groups and Study Design
L2221: The study consisted of 3 phases:
L2222: - Open-label phase (Week 0 to Week 16)
- Blinded active treatment phase (Week 16 through Week 44)
- Follow-up phase (Week 44 through Week 60)
L2223: Eligible subjects received open-label ustekinumab at Weeks 0 and 4. At week 16, efficacy was assessed using IGA which determined their subsequent treatment through Week 44.
L2224: - Subjects with an IGA \ge 2 (mild to severe disease, i.e., subjects with an inadequate response to ustekinumab) were randomized in a 1:1 fashion to eitherGuselkumab 100 mg at Weeks 16 and 20, then every 8 weeks (q8w) thereafter (i.e., weeks 28, 36, 44)
- Ustekinumab q 12w (i.e., weeks 16, 28, and 40)
- Subjects with an IGA = 0 or 1 (cleared or minimal disease) continued to receive open-label ustekinumab q12w from Week 16 through Week 40.
L2225: All subjects returned for a follow-up visit at Week 52 and a final safety visit at Week 60.
L2226: Database locks were planned for Weeks 40 and 60.
L2227: Figure 9 summarizes the study design.
L2228: Figure 9. Study Design (3003)
L2229: Key: IGA=Investigator's Global Assessment; W=week.
L2230: (Clinical Study Report, page 23 of 458)
L2231: ## 7.4.4. Disposition
L2232: A total of 1,105 subjects were screened, 872 subjects were randomized, and 871 subjects were treated with open-label ustekinumab 45 mg or 90 mg, according to the subject's baseline weight at Week 0. Subject disposition is summarized in Figure 10.
L2233: Figure 10. Subject Disposition (Study 3003)
L2234: Key: AEs=adverse events; FU=follow-up; IGA=Investigator's Global Assessment; w/draw=withdrawal
L2235: Extracted from: TSITG01 (n=1105, 233); TSITG02 (n=872, 871); TSIDS01a (n=9/135, 16/133, 268); TSIDS01c (n=17/585); LSIDS01 (n=18); LSIDS03 (n=135; n=133); LSIDS07 (n=1).
L2236: (Clinical Study Report, page 46 of 458)
L2237: ## 7.4.5. Demographics and Baseline Characteristics / Medical History
L2238: Hypertension (25.6%) and hyperlipidemia (12.9%) were the most common medical history diagnoses for the enrolled and treated subjects. A higher percentage of randomized subjects had diabetes in the ustekinumab treatment group (11.3%), compared to the guselkumab treatment group (3%).

L2327: **To:** BLA 761061

**Through:** Barbara Hill, Ph.D., Pharmacology/Toxicology Supervisor
L2328: ## **Re:**
L2329: **SDN:** 1
**Submission date:** 11-16-2016
**Submission type:** Original BLA
**Drug:** Guselkumab injection
**Indication:** Moderate-to-severe plaque psoriasis
**Route:** Subcutaneous injection
**Applicant:** Janssen Biotech, Inc.
L2330: The applicant submitted an original 351a BLA application for Guselkumab Injection indicated for the treatment of adult patients with moderate-to-severe plaque psoriasis who are candidates for systemic therapy or phototherapy. Guselkumab is a fully human immunoglobulin G1 lambda (IgG1\lambda) monoclonal antibody (mAb) directed against the p19 subunit of interleukin 23 (IL-23). The nonclinical pharmacology/toxicology review is complete and has been added to the Multi-disciplinary Review and Evaluation, which will be uploaded to DARRTS when it is finalized. Refer to the Multi-disciplinary Review and Evaluation for details.
L2331: Guselkumab is approvable for the treatment of moderate-to-severe plaque psoriasis from a Pharmacology/Toxicology perspective. There are no recommended nonclinical PMCs/PMRs for this BLA.

L2336: Bar chart showing the percent of subjects achieving IGA score of cleared (0) or minimal (1) at Week 28 across four quartiles of steady-state trough serum guselkumab concentration. The Y-axis is 'Percent of Subjects Achieving IGA Score of Cleared (0) or Minimal (1) at Week 28' (0 to 100). The X-axis is 'Serum Guselkumab Concentration (\mu\text{g/mL})'. The quartiles and corresponding response rates are: Quartile 1 (< 0.61 \mu\text{g/mL}): 77.7% (n=179); Quartile 2 (\ge 0.61 \text{ to } < 1.04 \mu\text{g/mL}): 89.0% (n=182); Quartile 3 (\ge 1.04 \text{ to } < 1.54 \mu\text{g/mL}): 89.4% (n=180); Quartile 4 (\ge 1.54 \mu\text{g/mL}): 94.5% (n=182).
L2337: Bar chart showing the percentage of responders (PGA 0/1 and PASI 90) at Week 16 for different guselkumab dose regimens in the Phase 2 study. The x-axis shows Placebo (n=42), 5 mg q12w (n=41), 15 mg q8w (n=41), 50 mg q12w (n=42), 100 mg q8w (n=42), and 200 mg q12w (n=42). The y-axis shows % responders (0 to 100). For PGA 0/1 (blue bars), response rates increase significantly with dose, peaking at 85% for 200 mg q12w. For PASI 90 responders (green bars), response rates also increase with dose, peaking at 65% for 200 mg q12w. The placebo group shows minimal response for both endpoints.
L2338: Line graph showing the time course of the percentage of subjects achieving PGA Scores of Cleared (0) or Minimal (1) across five different guselkumab dosing regimens (5 mg q12w, 15 mg q8w, 50 mg q12w, 100 mg q8w, and 200 mg q12w) from Week 4 to Week 40. The Y-axis represents the Percent of Subjects (0 to 100). The 100 mg q8w regimen consistently shows the highest percentage of subjects achieving PGA scores of Cleared (0) or Minimal (1), peaking around 65% at Week 28. The 50 mg q12w regimen also shows high efficacy, peaking around 58% at Week 20. The 5 mg q12w regimen shows the lowest efficacy, generally remaining below 20%.

Group IIIa (Nonresponders): Initiate guselkumab (●).
Group IIIb (Responders): Treatment withdrawal followed by guselkumab treatment upon loss of response (Gray shaded area).

L2360: Figure 24 illustrates the study scheme for PSO1001, detailing the dosing and randomization for Part 1 Healthy Subjects (N=47) and Part 2 Psoriasis Subjects (N=24).
Part 1 Healthy Subjects (N = 47)
The study involved two parts: IV administration (Part 1 IV) and SC administration (Part 1 SC).
Part 1 IV: Ascending single doses of IV CNTO 1959 (mg/kg) were administered sequentially: 0.03, 0.1, 0.3, 1, 3, and 10 mg/kg. Randomization ratios (Active:Placebo) were 3:1 for 0.03 and 0.1 mg/kg, and 6:2 for 0.3, 1, 3, and 10 mg/kg.
Part 1 SC: Healthy subjects were randomly assigned to a single SC dose of 3 mg/kg (6:1 ratio of Active:Placebo).
Part 2 Psoriasis Subjects (N = 24)
Subjects received SC CNTO 1959 (mg) doses: 10, 30, 100, and 300 mg. Randomization ratios (Active:Placebo) were 5:1 for all doses. Equivalent doses in mg/kg are 0.1, 0.3, 1.1, and 3.3 mg/kg, respectively.
L2361: Figure 25 displays two graphs showing Mean (SD) Serum guselkumab concentration (log scale) versus Time (Days) up to 112 days. The left panel, 'Healthy subjects (IV)', shows concentration profiles for IV doses of 0.03 mg/kg, 0.1 mg/kg, 0.3 mg/kg, 1 mg/kg, and 3 mg/kg. The right panel, 'Subjects with psoriasis (SC)', shows concentration profiles for SC doses of 10 mg, 30 mg, 100 mg, and 300 mg. Both graphs show a rapid decline in concentration over time.
L2362: Figure 26: Mean (SD) serum guselkumab concentration vs. time profiles following single SC administrations of guselkumab to Japanese subjects with psoriasis. The graph shows serum concentration (log scale, \mu\text{g/mL}) versus time (Days) for four doses: 10mg, 30mg, 100mg, and 300mg. Concentration decreases over time for all doses, with higher doses maintaining higher concentrations.

L2388: Timeline diagram illustrating the Study Design (3003) across Open-label, Active Treatment, and Follow-up phases, spanning Weeks 0 to 60.
Legend:
○ Guselkumab
□ Ustekinumab
X Placebo for guselkumab
| Placebo for ustekinumab
R = Randomization
DBL = Database lock
Study Flow:
Subjects were initially treated with open-label Ustekinumab (n=\sim800) from Week 0 to Week 16. At Week 16, subjects were assessed for Investigator's Global Assessment (IGA).
Randomized Subjects (IGA\ge2 at W16): Received Guselkumab 100 mg (n=130) during Active Treatment (Weeks 16 to 44).
Nonrandomized Subjects (IGA=0 or 1 at W16): Received Ustekinumab (n=520) during Active Treatment (Weeks 16 to 44).
During Active Treatment (Weeks 16 to 44), subjects were randomized (R) at Week 16. The randomized subjects were assigned to Guselkumab (n=130) or Ustekinumab (n=130). The nonrandomized subjects continued on Ustekinumab (n=520).
Database lock (DBL) occurred at Week 40 and Week 60.
